\"A shop specializing in drip coffee in Nakhon Ratchasima, Thailand\")\n\n  A shop specializing in drip coffee in [Nakhon Ratchasima](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakhon_Ratchasima \"Nakhon Ratchasima\"), Thailand\n* [![A café in a former church, Kobe, Japan](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/Kobe_Union_Church09s5s3200.jpg/250px-Kobe_Union_Church09s5s3200.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kobe_Union_Church09s5s3200.jpg \"A café in a former church, Kobe, Japan\")\n\n  A café in a former church, [Kobe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobe \"Kobe\"), Japan\n\n### Australia\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Coffeehouse&action=edit§ion=34 \"Edit section: Australia\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/db/Federal_Coffee_Palace_Melbourne.jpg/250px-Federal_Coffee_Palace_Melbourne.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Federal_Coffee_Palace_Melbourne.jpg)\n\nThe [Federal Coffee Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Coffee_Palace \"Federal Coffee Palace\"), built on Collins Street, Melbourne, in 1888, was the largest and grandest \"coffee palace\" ever built.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] It was demolished in 1973.\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Centre_Place_December_2012.jpg/250px-Centre_Place_December_2012.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Centre_Place_December_2012.jpg)\n\n[Centre Place, Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_Place,_Melbourne \"Centre Place, Melbourne\"). Australia and New Zealand have competing claims to being the birthplace of the \"[flat white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_white \"Flat white\")\".\n\nIn the 19th century, coffeehouses such as the [Federal Coffee Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_Coffee_Palace \"Federal Coffee Palace\") in the center of [Melbourne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melbourne \"Melbourne\") were part of the [temperance movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temperance_movement \"Temperance movement\").[[116]](#cite_note-120)\n\nIn modern Australia, coffee shops are commonly called cafés. Since the post-[World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II \"World War II\") influx of Italian and Greek immigrants introduced the first espresso machines to Australia in the 1950s, there was initially a slow rise in café culture, particularly in Melbourne, until a nationwide boom in locally-owned cafés began in the 1990s.[[117]](#cite_note-121) Alongside the rise in the number of cafés has been a rise in demand for locally (or on-site) roasted specialty coffee, particularly in [Sydney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sydney \"Sydney\") and Melbourne. A local favorite[*[dubious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement \"Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute\") – [discuss](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk:Coffeehouse#Dubious \"Talk:Coffeehouse\")*] is the \"[flat white](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_white \"Flat white\")\".[[118]](#cite_note-122)\n\n### Europe\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Coffeehouse&action=edit§ion=35 \"Edit section: Europe\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/32_Terrassa_de_les_Moreres%2C_davant_el_Casal%2C_pg._Joan_Creus_Ca%C3%B1ellas_2_%28Salom%C3%B3%29.jpg/250px-32_Terrassa_de_les_Moreres%2C_davant_el_Casal%2C_pg._Joan_Creus_Ca%C3%B1ellas_2_%28Salom%C3%B3%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:32_Terrassa_de_les_Moreres,_davant_el_Casal,_pg._Joan_Creus_Cañellas_2_(Salomó).jpg)\n\nA [sidewalk cafe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sidewalk_cafe \"Sidewalk cafe\") in Spain\n\nIn most European countries, such as [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain \"Spain\"), [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria \"Austria\"), [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark \"Denmark\"), Germany, [Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norway \"Norway\"), [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden \"Sweden\"), [Portugal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portugal \"Portugal\"), and others, the term *café* means a restaurant primarily serving coffee, as well as [pastries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pastry \"Pastry\") such as [cakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cake \"Cake\"), [tarts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tart \"Tart\"), [pies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pie \"Pie\"), and [buns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bun \"Bun\"). Many cafés also serve light meals such as sandwiches. European cafés often have tables on the pavement (sidewalk) as well as indoors. Some, particularly in Southern Europe, also serve alcoholic drinks (e.g., wine). In the Netherlands and Belgium, a *café* is the equivalent of a bar, and also sells alcoholic drinks. In the Netherlands a *koffiehuis* serves coffee, while a \"[coffee shop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis_coffee_shops \"Cannabis coffee shops\")\" (the English term) sells \"soft\" drugs ([cannabis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannabis_(drug) \"Cannabis (drug)\") and [hashish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hashish \"Hashish\")) and is generally not allowed to sell alcoholic drinks. In France, most cafés serve as lunch restaurants in the day, and bars in the evening. They generally do not have pastries except in the mornings, when a [croissant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croissant \"Croissant\") or [pain au chocolat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pain_au_chocolat \"Pain au chocolat\") can be bought with breakfast coffee.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*]\n\nIn Italy, cafés are similar to those found in France and known as *bar*. They typically serve a variety of espresso coffee, cakes and alcoholic drinks. Bars in city centers usually have different prices for consumption at the bar and consumption at a table.[[119]](#cite_note-123)\n\n#### Ireland\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Coffeehouse&action=edit§ion=36 \"Edit section: Ireland\")]\n\nToday, the word *café* – also spelled *cafe*,[[e]](#cite_note-124) but always pronounced as two syllables – is used for most coffeehouses. It has also come to be used for a type of diner that offers cooked meals (again, without alcoholic beverages) which can be standalone or operating within shopping centres or department stores.\n\n#### United Kingdom\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Coffeehouse&action=edit§ion=37 \"Edit section: United Kingdom\")]\n\nThe patrons of the early English coffeehouses were far removed from those of modern Britain. Haunts for teenagers in particular, [Italian-run](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italians_in_the_United_Kingdom \"Italians in the United Kingdom\") espresso bars and their [formica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formica_(plastic) \"Formica (plastic)\")-topped tables were a feature of 1950s [Soho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soho \"Soho\") that provided a backdrop as well as a title for [Cliff Richard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cliff_Richard \"Cliff Richard\")'s 1960 film *[Expresso Bongo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expresso_Bongo \"Expresso Bongo\")*. The first of these was the Moka in [Frith Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frith_Street \"Frith Street\"), opened by [Gina Lollobrigida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gina_Lollobrigida \"Gina Lollobrigida\") in 1953.[[120]](#cite_note-ClassicCafes-125) The late 50s Soho cafes in the film have \"[an] exotic [Gaggia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaggia \"Gaggia\") [coffee machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espresso_machine \"Espresso machine\") ... [and] [Coke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coca-Cola \"Coca-Cola\"), [Pepsi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pepsi \"Pepsi\"), weak frothy coffee and a Suncrush orange fountain\";[[121]](#cite_note-Perry-126) they spread to other urban centers during the 1960s, providing affordable, warm places for young people to congregate and an ambience far removed from the global coffee bar standard set in the final decades of the century by chains such as [Starbucks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks \"Starbucks\") and [Pret a Manger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pret_a_Manger \"Pret a Manger\").[[121]](#cite_note-Perry-126)[[120]](#cite_note-ClassicCafes-125)\n\n### Espresso bar\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Coffeehouse&action=edit§ion=38 \"Edit section: Espresso bar\")]\n\n|  |\n| --- |\n| This section **needs [more citations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability \"Wikipedia:Verifiability\")**. Please help [improve this section](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Coffeehouse \"Special:EditPage/Coffeehouse\") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners \"Help:Referencing for beginners\"). Unsourced material may be challenged and [removed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence \"Wikipedia:Verifiability\"). *(October 2018)* *([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal \"Help:Maintenance template removal\"))* |\n\n [![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ae/Baliuagjf1696_08.JPG/250px-Baliuagjf1696_08.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Baliuagjf1696_08.JPG)\n\nInterior of an espresso bar in [Baliwag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baliwag \"Baliwag\"), Philippines\n\nThe **espresso bar** is a type of coffeehouse that specializes in coffee drinks made from [espresso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espresso \"Espresso\"). Originating in Italy, it has spread throughout the world in various forms. International chains include [Starbucks Coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Starbucks \"Starbucks\"), based in [Seattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seattle \"Seattle\"), U.S., and [Costa Coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Costa_Coffee \"Costa Coffee\"), based in [Loudwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loudwater,_Buckinghamshire \"Loudwater, Buckinghamshire\"), U.K. (the first and second largest coffeehouse chains respectively), although the espresso bar exists in some form throughout much of the world.\n\nThe espresso bar typically has a long counter with a high-yield [espresso machine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Espresso_machine \"Espresso machine\") (usually bean to cup machines, automatic or semiautomatic pump-type machine, although occasionally a manually operated lever-and-piston system) and a display case containing pastries and occasionally savory items such as sandwiches. In the traditional Italian bar, customers either order at the bar and consume their drinks standing or, if they wish to sit down and be served, are usually charged a higher price. In some bars there is an additional charge for drinks served at an outside table. In other countries, especially the United States, seating areas for customers to relax and work are provided free of charge. Some espresso bars also sell coffee paraphernalia, candy, and even music. North American espresso bars were also at the forefront of the proliferation of public [Wi-Fi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wi-Fi \"Wi-Fi\") access points to provide Internet services to people working on laptop computers.\n\nThe offerings at the typical espresso bar are generally quite Italianate in inspiration; [biscotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biscotti \"Biscotti\"), [cannoli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cannoli \"Cannoli\") and [pizzelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pizzelle \"Pizzelle\") are common traditional accompaniments to a [caffè latte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latte \"Latte\") or [cappuccino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cappuccino \"Cappuccino\"). Some espresso bars even offer alcoholic drinks such as grappa and sambuca. Nevertheless, typical pastries are not always strictly Italianate and common additions include [scones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scones \"Scones\"), [muffins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muffins \"Muffins\"), [croissants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croissant \"Croissant\"), and even [doughnuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doughnut \"Doughnut\"). There is usually a large selection of teas as well, and the North American espresso bar culture is responsible for the popularization of the Indian spiced tea drink [masala chai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masala_chai \"Masala chai\"). Iced drinks are also popular in some countries, including both iced tea and iced coffee as well as blended drinks such as Starbucks' [Frappucino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frappucino \"Frappucino\").\n\nA worker in an espresso bar is called a [barista](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barista \"Barista\"). This is a skilled position that requires familiarity with the drinks being made (often very elaborate, especially in North American-style espresso bars) and a reasonable facility with some equipment, as well as the usual customer service skills.\n\n## Gallery\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Coffeehouse&action=edit§ion=39 \"Edit section: Gallery\")]\n\n*       "title": "Hôtel du Centre Nice - Our 24/7 Services",
      "content": "### Laundry service\n\nDiscover\n\n## HUG CAFÉ\n\nHôtel de France Nice - HUG café Nice - coffee shop    Hôtel de France Nice - coffee latte art Nice center\n\nIt's our coffee shop. It's open every day from 7AM to 6PM (7PM in high season).\n\nEnjoy breakfast as well as lunch and snacks on our wide terrace facing Notre Dame Basilica. We offer sweet and savoury snacks to suit all diets : pork-free, gluten-free, lactose-free, vegan and vegetarian while remaining healthy and delicious. Our expert baristas create sublime (in taste and appearance) caffeinated and non-caffeinated speciality drinks.\n\nHotel guests receive a 10% discount on all orders.\n\nDiscover the Hug Café\n\nHôtel du Centre - Breakfast platter - quality & healthy \n\n## BREAKFAST (7.30 am -11.00 am) [...] # SERVICES\n\nAt the Hôtel du Centre, we love our guests as much as we love our beloved city.\n\nOur team is on hand 24/7 to welcome you, help you and share their best tips so that you can discover Nice like a local.\n\nOur breakfast is served every day in our coffee shop, the HUG Café.\n\nHôtel du Centre - Services\n\nHôtel du Centre - inside of the coffee shop - expert baristas \n\n### Breakfast\n\n### Breakfast\n\nDiscover\n\nHôtel de France - Reception on the ground floor \n\n### Reception\n\n### Reception\n\nDiscover\n\nHôtel du Centre - helpful staff \n\n### Conciergerie\n\n### Conciergerie\n\nDiscover\n\nHôtel de France - carpark partnership \n\n### Parking\n\n### Parking\n\nDiscover\n\nHôtel de France - laundry service available \n\n### Laundry service\n\n### Laundry service\n\nDiscover\n\n## HUG CAFÉ [...] ## CONCIERGERIE\n\nHôtel de France Nice - tailored recommandations    Hôtel de France Nice - stagings in the room - Champagne - snacks - beverages\n\nWe looooove Nice and the French Riviera and will be happy to share our tips and guide you according to your preferences - everyone is different, so we'll be keen to understand your tastes and tailor our recommendations for guided tours, restaurants, bars and much more.\n\nWant to make your stay unique ? Surprise your loved one with our birthday, romantic or Nice welcome options! Our team will be happy to help!\n\nHôtel de France Nice - partnership with private parking \n\n## PARKING",
      "score": 0.15598944,
      "raw_content": "# SERVICES\n\nAt the Hôtel du Centre, we love our guests as much as we love our beloved city.\n\nOur team is **on hand 24/7** to welcome you, help you and share their **best tips** so that you can discover Nice like a local.\n\nOur **breakfast** is served every day in our **coffee shop**, the HUG Café.\n\n![Hôtel du Centre - Services]()\n\n![Hôtel du Centre - inside of the coffee shop - expert baristas]() \n\n### Breakfast\n\n### Breakfast\n\n[Discover](/services#breakfast)\n\n![Hôtel de France - Reception on the ground floor]() \n\n### Reception\n\n### Reception\n\n[Discover](/services#reception)\n\n![Hôtel du Centre - helpful staff]() \n\n### Conciergerie\n\n### Conciergerie\n\n[Discover](/services#conciergerie)\n\n![Hôtel de France - carpark partnership]() \n\n### Parking\n\n### Parking\n\n[Discover](/services#parking)\n\n![Hôtel de France - laundry service available]() \n\n### Laundry service\n\n### Laundry service\n\n[Discover](/services#laundry-service)\n\n## HUG CAFÉ\n\n![Hôtel de France Nice - HUG café Nice - coffee shop]()    ![Hôtel de France Nice - coffee latte art Nice center]()\n\nIt's our coffee shop. It's open every day from 7AM to 6PM (7PM in high season).\n\nEnjoy breakfast as well as lunch and snacks on our wide terrace facing Notre Dame Basilica. We offer sweet and savoury snacks to suit all diets : pork-free, gluten-free, lactose-free, vegan and vegetarian while remaining healthy and delicious. Our expert baristas create sublime (in taste and appearance) caffeinated and non-caffeinated speciality drinks.\n\n**Hotel guests receive a 10% discount on all orders.**\n\n[Discover the Hug Café](https://www.hugcafenice.com/)\n\n![Hôtel du Centre - Breakfast platter - quality & healthy]() \n\n## BREAKFAST (7.30 am -11.00 am)\n\nDuring your stay at Hôtel du Centre, enjoy breakfast at the **Restaurant Le Central**, located just **next to our hotel**. We offer **three breakfast options** made with **high-quality products** (organic eggs, Basque ham, 18-month aged Comté cheese, pastries from La Boulangerie Par Michel Fiori…).\n\n* **Express Breakfast**: Hot drink, freshly squeezed orange juice, and a pastry of your choice.\n* **Full Breakfast**: Hot drink, freshly squeezed orange juice, a pastry of your choice, organic egg prepared to your liking (fried, soft-boiled, or poached), with ham & 18-month aged Comté cheese.\n* **Gourmet Breakfast**: Hot drink, freshly squeezed orange juice, and 4 à-la-carte items of your choice from: Avocado Toast, French Toast, Grilled Bacon, Seasonal Fruits, Plain or Ham & Cheese Omelette, Organic Eggs & Pastries of your choice, White Ham & Comté cheese.\n\n## RECEPTION\n\nOur reception is **open 24/7**. Our reception team will treat you like an AMMI (a friend). They'll be on hand to **advise** you (from train timetables to bus destinations, from small bistro to trendy bar, on guided tours or walks...) to answer your questions, react if anything goes wrong, help you...\n\nBecause we are committed to satisfying everyone's needs, we have a number **of additional facilities available free of charge on request**, such as different types of pillow and bed linen, hygiene and ironing product kits, etc. Your luggage is safe with us if you wish to leave it with us while you wait for your room to be ready or after you have checked out.\n\n   ![Hôtel de France Nice - personalised welcome]()\n\n## CONCIERGERIE\n\n![Hôtel de France Nice - tailored recommandations]()    ![Hôtel de France Nice - stagings in the room - Champagne - snacks - beverages]()\n\nWe looooove Nice and the French Riviera and will be happy to **share our tips** and guide you **according to your preferences** - everyone is different, so we'll be keen to **understand your tastes** and **tailor our recommendations** for guided tours, restaurants, bars and much more.\n\nWant to make your stay **unique** ? **Surprise** your loved one with our birthday, romantic or Nice welcome options! Our team will be happy to help!\n\n![Hôtel de France Nice - partnership with private parking]() \n\n## PARKING\n\nWe have a **partnership with the Q-Park Notre Dame** car park at 28, rue Notre Dame, located 150m or 2 minutes' walk from the hotel. Go and park directly, then buy your discounted payment tickets at reception:\n\n* 12h = 18€\n* 24h = 27€ instead of 42€\n\n## LAUNDRY SERVICE\n\nMake yourself at home at AMMI Hotels and ask at reception for the **pillow of your dreams**.\n\nChoose from our memory foam pillows, bolsters, feather pillows and more.\n\n   ![Hôtel de France Nice - Services]()\n\n## OTHER SERVICES\n\n![Luggage storage]()\n\nLuggage storage\n\n![Courtesy shower]()\n\nCourtesy shower\n\n![Pets welcomed]()\n\nPets welcomed\n\n![Baby equipment]()\n\nBaby equipment\n\n![Books & games]()\n\nBooks & games\n\n* [en](#)\n  + [fr](/fr/services)\n  + [it](/it/Servizi)\n  + [de](/de/services)\n* + [![Templates WEBCOM360 - Gestion des Widgets](/cache/img/af4dcd5da2b89d4880f7aabf3bc3e4eca476fa18-af4dcd-original.svg)\n\n    +33 4 93 82 29 80](tel:+33493822980)\n  + [![Templates WEBCOM360 - Gestion des Widgets](/cache/img/0801c61d23e41652f45bf6e8afce9b70cbb1fc76-0801c6-original.svg)](https://wa.me/33493822980)\n  + [![Templates WEBCOM360 - Gestion des Widgets](/cache/img/c93127d5945a7fa3c88320dc71dbda6a80fe1939-c93127-original.svg)\n\n    contact@hotelducentre-nice.com](mailto:contact@hotelducentre-nice.com)\n  + [![Templates WEBCOM360 - Gestion des Widgets](/cache/img/7b036e78a16b38b9b80784758aa6184761d03c36-7b036e-original.svg)](https://www.instagram.com/hotelducentrenice/)\n  + [![Templates WEBCOM360 - Gestion des Widgets](/cache/img/daec57d45243250b7aa915acac1a43dd955096dc-daec57-original.svg)](https://www.facebook.com/NiceHotelduCentre/)\n\n[![Hôtel du Centre - SALE09](/cache/img/hotel-du-centre-sale09-269063-1920-1280-landscape.png)](https://www.secure-hotel-booking.com/Hotel-du-Centre/2CZJ/en?hotelId=19197&promoCode=WEB)\n\n [Save up to 20% by booking on our website.](https://www.secure-hotel-booking.com/Hotel-du-Centre/2CZJ/en?hotelId=19197&promoCode=WEB)\n ",

      "raw_content": "[![Manhattan Sideways logo](/build/_assets/manhattan-sideways-logo-3BVIH756.webp)](/)\n\n[About us](/about-us)[Partner with us](/partner-with-us)[Listen to our podcasts](https://www.podserve.fm/series/website/the-manhattan-sideways-podcast,268/)\n\n# Nice Matin\n\n###### Opening Hours\n\n**Today: 8am–3:30pm,5–11pm**\n\nSat:\n\n8am–3:30pm,5–11pm\n\nSun:\n\n8am–3:30pm,5–10pm\n\nMon:\n\n8am–3:30pm,5–10pm\n\nTues:\n\n8am–3:30pm,5–10pm\n\nWed:\n\n8am–3:30pm,5–10pm\n\nThurs:\n\n8am–3:30pm,5–10pm\n\n###### Location\n\n201 West 79th Street\n\n###### Contact\n\n[nicematinnyc.com](https://nicematinnyc.com/)[212 873 6423](tel:2128736423)\n\n###### Categories\n\n###### Neighborhoods\n\nThe inside of Nice Matin is like a bright carnival ground with three central pillars decorated with lights, wide spaces between tables, and colorful art on the walls. Above the doorway, there is an arrangement of baskets and fruit with handwritten signs and prices in euros, emulating European markets. At any hour of the day or evening, the restaurant is filled with people both from the surrounding neighborhood as well as from the Lucerne, the connecting hotel.  \n  \nThe chef and owner of Nice Matin is Andy D'Amico, who has since gone on to develop other restaurants around the city, including 5 Napkin Burger. After attending the Culinary Institute of America, Andy worked at Parker House in Boston and at the Sign of the Dove on the Upper East Side, where he was promoted to head chef. In 2003, he partnered with Simon Oren to open Nice Matin, a venture that earned him the title of \"Best Chef 2003\" from New York Magazine.  \n  \nI met with Danny, the General Manager, who shared their elaborate cocktail list that had classics with a twist. He brought out a San Tropez, Nice Matin's take on the mojito. It had summery passion fruit foam that complemented the mint and dark rum. The restaurant has seasonal cocktails, such as hot toddies in the winter and Campari cocktails in the spring. My favorite time to dine at Nice Matin, however, is on a warm weekend morning when their elaborate brunch menu is offered, and I can sit outside.\n\nShare:\n\n[Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/sharer/sharer.php?u=https://sideways.nyc/discover/1DSWtWJkTkKnSA4e0vrxtZ/nice-matin)\n\n[Twitter](https://twitter.com/intent/tweet?text=https://sideways.nyc/discover/1DSWtWJkTkKnSA4e0vrxtZ/nice-matin)\n\n[Pinterest](https://pinterest.com/pin/create/button/?url=https://sideways.nyc/discover/1DSWtWJkTkKnSA4e0vrxtZ/nice-matin)\n\n[Email](mailto:?subject=Nice Matin&body=https://sideways.nyc/discover/1DSWtWJkTkKnSA4e0vrxtZ/nice-matin)\n\n###### Location\n\nLoading\n\n###### Sign up to Sidestreet Updates\n\n### More Breakfast nearby\n\nLost Gem\n\n[### Irving Farm Coffee Roasters\n\nWhen we visited Irving Farm late one morning, almost every seat was taken and there was a constant stream of people ordering cappuccinos and breakfast sandwiches. \"This is one of our busiest locations, \" Liz Dean, the manager, said, explaining that it functions as a flagship store. There was a wide demographic, from mothers and young children to groups of out of towners. \"This neighborhood is very interesting - we get a nice mix of regulars and tourists, \" Liz pointed out. Liz was a regular for a while before becoming a member of the team. Now that she does the hiring, she looks out for people like herself, people who understand what Irving Farms is about and are already invested in its product. \"I prefer to hire people who love our coffee, \" she admitted. Two former college friends, David Elwell and Steve Leven, founded the company in 1996. The roasting is done in a carriage house in Millerton, New York, and there are now five cafes scattered throughout the city, as well as a training \"Loft\" in Chelsea. Though the cafe on Irving Place is the original, as the name would imply, it was recently renovated to look more like the Upper West Side spot, which opened in 2012, making the 79th street store the model for future expansion. In addition, Irving Farms has wholesale clients throughout the country and there are two more cafes set to open - one on the Upper East Side and another at the Fulton Street station. Liz is particularly excited for the Upper East Side location, since many of her customers live or work on the other side of the park. While the coffee menu is the same at each of the Irving Farms locations, the food offerings change slightly. 79th offers a wide range of food, thanks to the size of its kitchen. Demba, the barista, appeared to be both a master of foam art and a friendly face to many. He greeted people by name, effortlessly remembering their regular orders. My daughter, Joelle, who was visiting on the day that we were walking on 79th, declared that her decaf latte was \"Rich and oh so good, \" and elaborated by saying it was a \"real cup of coffee, \" not what she was use to being served in the Boston area. She was also thrilled with her scrambled eggs on a homemade flaky cheddar cheese biscuit with avocado. Liz was pleased with Joelle's reaction, commenting, \"The breakfast sandwiches are huge here. \" Liz went on to say that the lattes and pour overs are also popular, but that coffee lovers equally enjoy trying the single origin coffees, which are rotated throughout the year. Irving Farm is part of the Specialty Coffee Association of America, but they try to go above and beyond the definition of \"specialty\" by not buying coffee that is graded less than 85 in the 100-point system. They consistently try to give back to the farmers as much as possible, including featuring profiles of the farmers on their website. \"In the city, it's so easy for there to be a disconnect between you and what you consume, \" Liz said after speaking to us about her trip to El Salvador, where the ethics behind coffee were really driven home. People often ask Liz if she feels threatened by Starbucks, and her simple answer is \"Absolutely not. \" Liz and her co-workers know that Irving Farms is targeting customers who can taste and appreciate the difference. \"We are, after all, a coffee roasting company first and foremost. \"Yes, they are known for their coffee, and people are constantly stopping in for a fresh cup. However, I also appreciate that later in the afternoon, Irving Farm has a different vibe. Wine and beer alongside cheese boards are brought out, and people are given the opportunity to relax and participate in pleasant, quiet conversation while unwinding from the day.](/discover/6JSP43bYCaJU4xLAHL6Fk4/irving-farm)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[![Serafina](/build/_assets/placeholder-shop-5I2DNTIU.webp)](/discover/1Zn4BXAxKeEFEMzmaV0Xa/serafina-4)\n\n[### Serafina](/discover/1Zn4BXAxKeEFEMzmaV0Xa/serafina-4)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[![French Roast](/build/_assets/placeholder-shop-5I2DNTIU.webp)](/discover/7chG2rt03O9JpwRHa4cQa0/french-roast-2)\n\n[### French Roast](/discover/7chG2rt03O9JpwRHa4cQa0/french-roast-2)\n\n### More places on 79th Street\n\nLost Gem\n\n[### Sojourn\n\nSojourn calls itself the Upper East Side’s “sexiest restaurant, ” and it is hard to argue: the color scheme, in coppers browns and reds, gives the restaurant a warm, intimate feeling. The name, which means “a temporary stay, ” hints at the fact that visitors can expect a full dining experience. Olivia, a member of the Manhattan Sideways team, was excited to return to Sojourn. She and her family had discovered the restaurant, tucked behind a residential-looking doorway, right before Thanksgiving and had visited two more times by the New Year. Along with the friendly staff, warm ambience, and delectable, seasonal food, what makes Sojourn stand out is its approach to courses: all menu items can be ordered as sharable tapas, with just the right number for the table. For example, when Olivia went with a group of seven family members and ordered the chorizo croquettes, the waiter said he would bring out two orders at three to a plate... plus one extra. Using this innovative way of ordering, each party can essentially create their own tasting menu. As for beverages, the cocktail menu is sophisticated and diverse. The restaurant not only has a large selection of wine, but also keeps some of their grapes in barrels rather than bottles, a more environmentally friendly method of storing and serving it. Among the many menu items that Olivia’s family tasted were the zesty arugula salad, crispy fish tacos, and Kobe beef sliders. Despite being thoroughly full, they made sure to have enough room for the warm, fluffy churros served with Mexican chocolate dipping sauce. We spoke to Johnny Musovic, who owns Sojourn with his father, Sami. They originally opened a Mexican restaurant called Santa Fe in the same location, but discovered that the neighborhood did not have a strong need for casual Mexican food. Instead, the father and son duo reopened with a higher-end concept which has been wholly embraced. Johnny proudly told me that his father is no newcomer to the restaurant world, having been the Head Maitre D’ at Sparks Steakhouse and Mr. Chow’s. He also has two other restaurants nearby. As for Johnny himself, he told me “In this industry, you can’t be afraid to get your hands dirty, ” referencing his time spent as everything from dishwasher to delivery boy to co-owner. He is clearly very proud of Sojourn for a variety of reasons, beginning with the food. “Most chefs are into fresh, local ingredients, but these chefs really are. ” He is also happy to have cultivated a chic, relaxing space, which includes live music on Monday and Tuesday evenings. Though he proclaims that the Upper East Side is his favorite part of the city, Johnny’s dream is to open up a Sojourn in Midtown one day. Until then, his goal is to integrate his bar crowd and his dining crowd. One night, he held a two hour open bar as his way of “giving back” to the neighborhood. Along with drinks, he offered his customers a series of hors d’oeuvres. He was surprised by how many of his bar regulars approached him and said, “I didn’t realize you had such great food! ”](/discover/1MYlPha0Vd8UgRZuKrXyil/sojourn)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[### First Baptist Church](/discover/1WP3K2np4GJoJ5AdEKyvXn/first-baptist-church)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[### Temple Shaaray Tefila\n\nShaaray Tefila has a very special place in my heart. For well over twenty years, beginning in the early 1970's, this was a home away from home for my grandparents. Reaching 79th Street and having the opportunity to write about this synagogue has brought tears to my eyes again and again. Rabbi Tattelbaum played an important role not only in my grandparent's lives, but in mine as well, when I was a young, impressionable teenager. It was Chip Schrager, the Communications Coordinator for the temple in 2015, who kindly guided the Manhattan Sideways team through the space, beginning with the main sanctuary. The room is expansive, seating 400 people downstairs and 200 in the balcony, and Chip was proud to say that it was filled to the rafters during the recent Hanukkah services. Something that I did not know was that the building used to be a movie theater until the temple took over in 1958. The old projector room is now used as an office for the parenting programs. Founded in 1845 as a strict Orthodox temple, Shaaray Tefila has shifted locations throughout the city, becoming Reform along the way. Stepping into the chapel, where smaller services are held, I saw bold stained glass ornaments on one side of the room with the names and symbols of characters from Jewish lore. In the meeting room nearby, well-polished Judaic pieces, along with artifacts dating back to the temple's founding were displayed. In addition, we took note of photographs of the old temple on West 82nd Street, the Seal of the Congregation, and even the trowel that the rabbi used to lay the cornerstone of the Temple. Leaving the room, Chip gestured to photographs of six men who were senior rabbis at Temple Shaaray Tefila. The temple has a strong children's program, including a nursery school, kindergarten, and religious school that extends through high school. We appreciated getting to observe the room used for art class. A giant paint pallet decorated the wall and colorful supplies lined the room. We then ventured up to the roof where the playground is located, surrounded by a fence that still allowed for a beautiful view of the winter sunset. It was here that Chip continued to speak of the various programs offered to every age group, including senior citizens. This is what my grandparents took advantage of so many years ago, and it warmed my heart to know that people are still participating in the various classes that Shaaray Tefila has to offer. As Chip beautifully stated, \"Whatever your Jewish journey is, we want to be a part of it. \"](/discover/2X2DzamRKgTF5z74XYlEVZ/temple-shaaray-tefila)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[### The Lucerne](/discover/459lD00L3ioh25EkLKmvsA/lucerne)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[### Imperial Court Hotel](/discover/47Oi6r2O4eI5DL1HFf8Li2/imperial-court-hotel)\n\n### More French nearby\n\nLost Gem\n\n[![Essential by Christophe](/build/_assets/placeholder-shop-5I2DNTIU.webp)](/discover/3hG8oIdb86HljTnI1odYiQ/essential-by-christophe)\n\n[### Essential by Christophe](/discover/3hG8oIdb86HljTnI1odYiQ/essential-by-christophe)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[![French Roast](/build/_assets/placeholder-shop-5I2DNTIU.webp)](/discover/7chG2rt03O9JpwRHa4cQa0/french-roast-2)\n\n[### French Roast](/discover/7chG2rt03O9JpwRHa4cQa0/french-roast-2)\n\nLost Gem\n\n[![Maison Pickle](/build/_assets/placeholder-shop-5I2DNTIU.webp)](/discover/vRZYM7klRWVvlmYlZ0CO1/maison-pickle)\n\n[### Maison Pickle](/discover/vRZYM7klRWVvlmYlZ0CO1/maison-pickle)",

      "title": "Cocktail Bar Nice I Le 1913",
      "content": "## Gallery\n\nLe-Negresco-cClaesBech-PoulseHD-bar-4.jpg\nDesign-sans-titre-8-1.png\nLe-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-384-2.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-927.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2459-HD-1.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2792-HD.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2303-HD.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2752-HD.jpg\nle-Negresco-Le-Bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-689.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Virginie-Basselot-cGregoire-Gardette-827-BD.jpg\nGregoireGardette-BD-46-1.jpg\nLe-Negresco-Salon-Versailles-30-c-Gregoire-Gardette-BD-1.jpg\n\n### Virginie Basselot [...] ### Virginie Basselot\n\nFree-spirited and passionate, Virginie Basselot represents a new generation of chefs who are redefining the contours of haute cuisine with audacity, precision, and a profoundly human touch. Her culinary vision is sincere and committed; each dish tells a story of flavour, of place, and of the art of transmission.\n\n### Local Produce and Savoir-Faire [...] ### Local Produce and Savoir-Faire\n\nVirginie Basselot draws her inspiration from the heart of the Mediterranean, where the excellence of local produce takes centre stage. Freshness, seasonality, and proximity are the guiding principles of this Meilleur Ouvrier de France, who elevates each ingredient with elegance, audacity, and technical mastery. Around her, a passionate brigade – from her sous-chefs Michelle Gibon and Amélia Losilla to chef de partie – brings to life a vibrant Mediterranean: iodine-rich, plant-filled, and earthy. Each plate pays homage to the rich traditions of Nice, with deep respect for its heritage and the momentum of a resolutely contemporary creativity.\n\n### An ever-present Heritage",
      "score": 0.12055289,
      "raw_content": "Member of The Leading Hotels of the World\n\n37 Promenade des Anglais, 06000 Nice France\n\n# Le 1913\n\n![Le-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-384-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-384-1.jpg)\n![le-Negresco-Le-Bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-652-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/le-Negresco-Le-Bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-652-1.jpg)\n\n## Le 1913\n\n### The historic bar at Le Negresco evokes the cosy and elegant atmosphere of the English clubs once favoured by dandies and elegant society women.\n\nIts timeless décor featuring wood panelling, nineteenth-century wall lights, and plush upholstered Louis XVI armchairs has remained unchanged since 1913, when Le Negresco was inaugurated.\n\n![Le-Negresco-Le-bar-Gin-locaux-c-Gregoire-Gardette-852.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-bar-Gin-locaux-c-Gregoire-Gardette-852.jpg)\n![](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/N-LES-BARS_PANTONE.png)\n![Design-sans-titre-4.png](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Design-sans-titre-4.png)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-1913-c-Gregoire-Gardette-1151-BD.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-1913-c-Gregoire-Gardette-1151-BD.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-BarcGregoire-Gardette036-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-BarcGregoire-Gardette036-1.jpg)\n\nA scent of oak and amber wafts around the artworks, which seem to have found the most perfect setting. A marble bust of Queen Marie-Antoinette (1755-1793) with her hair plaited, a 1683 Brussels tapestry entitled Le Temps enchaîné par l’Amour (Time Chained by Love) and a stunning crystal whisky fountain take centre stage.\n\nOur Head Barman performs his magic with his team preparing drinks, and enquiring about guests’ preferences before conducting an impeccably attuned and precise ballet. This is the art of the mise-en-scène, preparing and dressing up creations. Lovers of art and elegance will not resist the temptation of a signature or non-alcoholic cocktail, a liqueur, a whisky, as they delve into the remarkable collection of spirits, accompanied by sweet strains of jazzy, pop or soul music five nights a week.\n\n### As the hours tick by, the ambience evolves. Mysterious and elegant by day, celebratory and informal by night. The chiaroscuro plays with light and shadow, providing a perfect backdrop for night owls seeking the ultimate refinement.\n\n## Gallery\n\n![Le-Negresco-cClaesBech-PoulseHD-bar-4.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-cClaesBech-PoulseHD-bar-4.jpg)\n![Design-sans-titre-8-1.png](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Design-sans-titre-8-1.png)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-384-2.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-384-2.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-927.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-927.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2459-HD-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2459-HD-1.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2792-HD.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2792-HD.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2303-HD.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2303-HD.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2752-HD.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2752-HD.jpg)\n![le-Negresco-Le-Bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-689.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/le-Negresco-Le-Bar-c-Gregoire-Gardette-689.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Virginie-Basselot-cGregoire-Gardette-827-BD.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/Le-Negresco-Virginie-Basselot-cGregoire-Gardette-827-BD.jpg)\n![GregoireGardette-BD-46-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/GregoireGardette-BD-46-1.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Salon-Versailles-30-c-Gregoire-Gardette-BD-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Le-Negresco-Salon-Versailles-30-c-Gregoire-Gardette-BD-1.jpg)\n\n### Virginie Basselot\n\nFree-spirited and passionate, Virginie Basselot represents a new generation of chefs who are redefining the contours of haute cuisine with audacity, precision, and a profoundly human touch. Her culinary vision is sincere and committed; each dish tells a story of flavour, of place, and of the art of transmission.\n\n### Local Produce and Savoir-Faire\n\nVirginie Basselot draws her inspiration from the heart of the Mediterranean, where the excellence of local produce takes centre stage. Freshness, seasonality, and proximity are the guiding principles of this Meilleur Ouvrier de France, who elevates each ingredient with elegance, audacity, and technical mastery. Around her, a passionate brigade – from her sous-chefs Michelle Gibon and Amélia Losilla to chef de partie – brings to life a vibrant Mediterranean: iodine-rich, plant-filled, and earthy. Each plate pays homage to the rich traditions of Nice, with deep respect for its heritage and the momentum of a resolutely contemporary creativity.\n\n### An ever-present Heritage\n\nIn 1957, the visionary and passionate Jeanne Augier transformed Le Negresco into a hotel of art and history. Through rare pieces and majestic styles, she created a unique masterpiece on the Riviera. At the heart of Le Chantecler and the Salon Régence, art graces the table: a portrait of Louis XV by La Tour, Marie Leszczynska by Nattier, wood panelling from the Château de Chaintré, or leatherwork from Malines… Every detail reflects the elegance of the Grand Siècle and the finest expression of French excellence.\n\n![LeNegresco-LeChantecler-ServicecGregoireGardette-bd-6.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/LeNegresco-LeChantecler-ServicecGregoireGardette-bd-6.jpg)\n![GregoireGardette-HD-6-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/GregoireGardette-HD-6-1.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-94-c-Gregoire-Gardette-BD-1.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Le-Negresco-94-c-Gregoire-Gardette-BD-1.jpg)\n![Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2459-HD-3-2.jpg](https://mozcp.lenegresco.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/06/Le-Negresco-Le-1913-cGregoire-Gardette-2459-HD-3-2.jpg)\n\nOctober
